People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. And then, weirdly, my belt’s been sitting a notch tighter than it used to, so… I don’t know if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d actually do anything to hurt myself. I’m not making a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, sometimes the thought pops up more than I’d like to admit, like maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up one morning. I know that sounds bad. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I can still get things done, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s definitely harder than it used to be.\n\nAt work, I used to knock out sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly, and now I’ll catch myself reading the same slide five times. I second-guess every number, and then I still can’t decide how to phrase what I’m trying to say. It’s frustrating… and honestly a little embarrassing. Is that what you mean by concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking noticeably slower lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think people at work would say I’m, you know, falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up and getting through meetings.\n\nBut… I can tell I’m slower. I’ll sit there staring at my laptop longer than I should, like I’m trying to get myself to start moving. And sometimes one of my reps asks me something simple and I take a beat too long to answer. Maybe it’s not super obvious to everyone, but I notice it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control the worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worrying exactly… or, I mean, maybe some of it is. When it starts, I can usually push through what I need to do, but it can be hard to just switch it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like it keeps circling in the background, and I have to kind of talk myself out of it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “unusually” tired… I mean, everybody’s tired, right? But yeah, I guess my energy hasn’t been great lately.\n\nI can still get through the workday, so it’s not like I’m unable to function. It’s more that things feel like they take more effort than they should, and by the time I’m done, I’m pretty drained. Does that count as low energy?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I still do some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. Like I’m going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to look forward to pickup basketball, bowling nights, music festivals, and firing up the smoker for friends, but lately I either cancel or show up and feel like I'm just going through the motions.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: My appetite has been off for months, where I’ll run on coffee through lunch, pick at dinner with my family, and then realize my belt is sitting a notch tighter than it used to.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: What finally made me call the employee assistance program is that I’m barely sleeping right—I lie there replaying work and arguments at home, wake up around 3 or 4, and then start the day feeling like I never really rested.\n- I move slower, sit staring at my laptop longer, and sometimes take a beat too long to answer my reps when they ask me something simple.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying every late night at work, every tailgate, every dumb purchase, and I end up thinking my wife and kids got stuck with a selfish guy who keeps letting them down.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I used to knock out sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly, but now I read the same slide five times, second-guess every number, and still can’t decide what to say.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I’m not making a plan to hurt myself, but the thought comes up more than I want to admit that maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up one morning.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’m still showing up, but my team has had to cover gaps when my forecasts and client prep slip, and at home I’m less present with What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess what finally pushed me to call the employee assistance program was my sleep. It’s probably not that dramatic, but I’m just not sleeping right. I lie there replaying work stuff, or arguments at home, and then I wake up around 3 or 4 and can’t really get back to sleep. Then I start the day feeling like I didn’t rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about many different things, not just sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worrying about everything, exactly. I mean, I think about work and home stuff more than I probably should, but it’s not like I’m panicking all day or anything. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Did your sleep problems start after a specific stressful event or change in your life?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. It wasn’t like one big event exactly, but it kind of started after things at home got more tense. The arguments started getting a lot worse, and… I guess it’s been going on maybe a year and a couple months now. About a year and two months, if I’m being honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down or depressed much of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately there’s just this kind of heavy, gray feeling that sticks with me. Like I’ll leave the office, get in the car, go home, and it’s still there. Even when nothing specific is happening, I feel kind of… hollow, I guess. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, I still do some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to—pickup basketball, bowling nights, going to music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker—lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, you know? Like I’m going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, it’s not like I’m not eating at all or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off for months. I’ll get through the day on coffee and realize I skipped lunch, then at dinner with my family I mostly just pick at my food. And then, weirdly, my belt’s been sitting a notch tighter than it used to, so… I don’t know if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d actually do anything to hurt myself. I’m not making a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, sometimes the thought pops up more than I’d like to admit, like maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up one morning. I know that sounds bad. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I can still get things done, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s definitely harder than it used to be.\n\nAt work, I used to knock out sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly, and now I’ll catch myself reading the same slide five times. I second-guess every number, and then I still can’t decide how to phrase what I’m trying to say. It’s frustrating… and honestly a little embarrassing. Is that what you mean by concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking noticeably slower lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think people at work would say I’m, you know, falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up and getting through meetings.\n\nBut… I can tell I’m slower. I’ll sit there staring at my laptop longer than I should, like I’m trying to get myself to start moving. And sometimes one of my reps asks me something simple and I take a beat too long to answer. Maybe it’s not super obvious to everyone, but I notice it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control the worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worrying exactly… or, I mean, maybe some of it is. When it starts, I can usually push through what I need to do, but it can be hard to just switch it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like it keeps circling in the background, and I have to kind of talk myself out of it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “unusually” tired… I mean, everybody’s tired, right? But yeah, I guess my energy hasn’t been great lately.\n\nI can still get through the workday, so it’s not like I’m unable to function. It’s more that things feel like they take more effort than they should, and by the time I’m done, I’m pretty drained. Does that count as low energy?\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I feel worthless all the time or anything. That sounds kind of dramatic.\n\nBut I do get stuck replaying things I feel bad about. Like all the late nights at work, or times I was at a tailgate instead of being more present, or even dumb stuff I bought that I probably shouldn’t have. And then it turns into this thought that my wife and kids got stuck with this selfish guy who keeps letting them down.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I’m not trying to make myself out to be a monster, but… yeah, I do feel guilty about it more than I’d like to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt unusually up, energized, or irritable for days at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, at least.\n\nI’ve had good days where I get more done, or times I’m kind of keyed up from work stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt unusually up or energized. Irritable maybe here and there, but nothing that lasted for days or felt out of character, I don’t think.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you can’t fall back asleep at 3 or 4 in the morning, is your mind usually busy with thoughts, or do you feel awake even without anything specific on your mind?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s thoughts, yeah. It wasn’t like one big event exactly, but it kind of started after things at home got more tense. The arguments started getting a lot worse, and… I guess it’s been going on maybe a year and a couple months now. About a year and two months, if I’m being honest. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Over the past couple of weeks, have you been feeling down or depressed much of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but most days lately there’s just this kind of heavy, gray feeling that sticks with me. Like I’ll leave the office, get in the car, go home, and it’s still there. Even when nothing specific is happening, I feel kind of… hollow, I guess. Does that count as depressed?\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things less than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… probably. I mean, I still do some things, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely stopped living or anything. But stuff I used to really look forward to—pickup basketball, bowling nights, going to music festivals, having people over and firing up the smoker—lately I’ll either cancel, or I’ll go and just feel like I’m kind of… there, you know? Like I’m going through the motions more than actually enjoying it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a change in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah. I mean, it’s not like I’m not eating at all or anything, but my appetite’s been kind of off for months. I’ll get through the day on coffee and realize I skipped lunch, then at dinner with my family I mostly just pick at my food. And then, weirdly, my belt’s been sitting a notch tighter than it used to, so… I don’t know if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d actually do anything to hurt myself. I’m not making a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, sometimes the thought pops up more than I’d like to admit, like maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up one morning. I know that sounds bad. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I can still get things done, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s definitely harder than it used to be.\n\nAt work, I used to knock out sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly, and now I’ll catch myself reading the same slide five times. I second-guess every number, and then I still can’t decide how to phrase what I’m trying to say. It’s frustrating… and honestly a little embarrassing. Is that what you mean by concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking noticeably slower lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think people at work would say I’m, you know, falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up and getting through meetings.\n\nBut… I can tell I’m slower. I’ll sit there staring at my laptop longer than I should, like I’m trying to get myself to start moving. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control the worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worrying exactly… or, I mean, maybe some of it is. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt unusually up, energized, or irritable for days at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, at least.\n\nI’ve had good days where I get more done, or times I’m kind of keyed up from work stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt unusually up or energized. Irritable maybe here and there, but nothing that lasted for days or felt out of character, I don’t think.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you can’t fall back asleep at 3 or 4 in the morning, is your mind usually busy with thoughts, or do you feel awake even without anything specific on your mind?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s thoughts, yeah. I mean, sometimes I’m just awake, but most of the time my head sort of clicks on and I start replaying work stuff, or arguments at home, things I said or should’ve handled better.\n\nThat’s actually part of what made me finally call the employee assistance program. I wasn’t sleeping right — I’d wake up around 3 or 4 and just lie there, and then start the day feeling like I never really rested. I don’t know if that’s the kind of detail you’re looking for, but that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood and loss of interest symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s definitely been longer than two weeks.\n\nIt’s probably been going on for, I guess, about a year and a couple months now — since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. Some days are better than others, but most days it’s been kind of there in the background. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before the sleep problems started, were you already feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I was. I mean, I probably would’ve called it “stressed” at the time, because that sounds better, I guess.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is happening, I’ll just feel kind of hollow.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way, but… yeah, I think it was already there.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you been worrying excessively about a number of different things on most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’d call it “excessive,” honestly. I mean, I do worry about things, like anyone does, but… a number of different things most days? I don’t know.\n\nIt’s more like I get stuck on certain things when they come up, if that makes sense. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: On most days lately, have you felt keyed up or unable to relax?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a constant way, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I have moments where I feel tense or like I should be doing something, but “keyed up” sounds a little stronger than what I’d say. I’ll get through the day on coffee and realize I skipped lunch, then at dinner with my family I mostly just pick at my food. And then, weirdly, my belt’s been sitting a notch tighter than it used to, so… I don’t know if that’s the kind of change you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I’d actually do anything to hurt myself. I’m not making a plan or anything like that.\n\nBut… yeah, sometimes the thought pops up more than I’d like to admit, like maybe everyone would be better off if I just didn’t wake up one morning. I know that sounds bad. I don’t want you to think I’m trying to be dramatic or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I can still get things done, so I don’t want to overstate it. But it’s definitely harder than it used to be.\n\nAt work, I used to knock out sales forecasts and client decks pretty quickly, and now I’ll catch myself reading the same slide five times. I second-guess every number, and then I still can’t decide how to phrase what I’m trying to say. It’s frustrating… and honestly a little embarrassing. Is that what you mean by concentrating?\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking noticeably slower lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think people at work would say I’m, you know, falling apart or anything. I’m still showing up and getting through meetings.\n\nBut… I can tell I’m slower. I’ll sit there staring at my laptop longer than I should, like I’m trying to get myself to start moving. And sometimes one of my reps asks me something simple and I take a beat too long to answer. Maybe it’s not super obvious to everyone, but I notice it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control the worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it worrying exactly… or, I mean, maybe some of it is. When it starts, I can usually push through what I need to do, but it can be hard to just switch it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like it keeps circling in the background, and I have to kind of talk myself out of it. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “unusually” tired… I mean, everybody’s tired, right? But yeah, I guess my energy hasn’t been great lately.\n\nI can still get through the workday, so it’s not like I’m unable to function. It’s more that things feel like they take more effort than they should, and by the time I’m done, I’m pretty drained. Does that count as low energy?\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been feeling worthless or excessively guilty?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I feel worthless all the time or anything. That sounds kind of dramatic.\n\nBut I do get stuck replaying things I feel bad about. Like all the late nights at work, or times I was at a tailgate instead of being more present, or even dumb stuff I bought that I probably shouldn’t have. And then it turns into this thought that my wife and kids got stuck with this selfish guy who keeps letting them down.\n\nI know that probably sounds harsh. I’m not trying to make myself out to be a monster, but… yeah, I do feel guilty about it more than I’d like to admit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period where you felt unusually up, energized, or irritable for days at a time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you mean, at least.\n\nI’ve had good days where I get more done, or times I’m kind of keyed up from work stuff, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had days where I felt unusually up or energized. Irritable maybe here and there, but nothing that lasted for days or felt out of character, I don’t think.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you can’t fall back asleep at 3 or 4 in the morning, is your mind usually busy with thoughts, or do you feel awake even without anything specific on your mind?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s thoughts, yeah. I mean, sometimes I’m just awake, but most of the time my head sort of clicks on and I start replaying work stuff, or arguments at home, things I said or should’ve handled better.\n\nThat’s actually part of what made me finally call the employee assistance program. I wasn’t sleeping right — I’d wake up around 3 or 4 and just lie there, and then start the day feeling like I never really rested. I don’t know if that’s the kind of detail you’re looking for, but that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have these low mood and loss of interest symptoms been present most days for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but it’s definitely been longer than two weeks.\n\nIt’s probably been going on for, I guess, about a year and a couple months now — since the arguments at home started getting a lot worse. Some days are better than others, but most days it’s been kind of there in the background. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Before the sleep problems started, were you already feeling down most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I was. I mean, I probably would’ve called it “stressed” at the time, because that sounds better, I guess.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, most days it’s like this heavy, gray mood just kind of follows me around — from the office to the car to the house. Even when nothing specific is happening, I’ll just feel kind of hollow.\n\nI don’t know if that answers it the right way, but… yeah, I think it was already there.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you been worrying excessively about a number of different things on most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure I’d call it “excessive,” honestly. I mean, I do worry about things, like anyone does, but… a number of different things most days? I don’t know.\n\nIt’s more like I get stuck on certain things when they come up, if that makes sense. I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Is that the kind of worrying you mean?\n\nDoctor: On most days lately, have you felt keyed up or unable to relax?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not really in a constant way, I don’t think.\n\nI mean, I have moments where I feel tense or like I should be doing something, but “keyed up” sounds a little stronger than what I’d say. I can usually sit still. Relaxing isn’t always easy, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound like I’m on edge most days, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Lately, have you been waking up earlier than you want to almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s actually been happening. I don’t know if I’d say every single day, but pretty close.\n\nI’ll fall asleep eventually, but then I’m up around 3 or 4 in the morning, and once I’m awake my mind just starts going — work stuff, things I said at home, replaying it all. Then I just kind of lie there until it’s time to get up.\n\nHonestly, that was part of what finally made me call the employee assistance program. I was getting through the day, but it felt like I wasn’t really rested. Does that count as waking up early the way you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
